REASONING_CONTENT
The node visitor already has `self.imports` - a set of module names imported. We could use that to build dependency graph. But it's built during
scanning. For cached files we won't have it unless stored. We can compute imports separately via a lightweight AST parse to store dependencies.

Actually the "Circular imports must not cause infinite loops" requirement strongly suggests there's a dependency graph. Let me think about why
dependency graph is needed at all for a security linter like Bandit.

Bandit plugin behavior can depend on imported modules - e.g., `bandit.core.context` resolves call names using `import_aliases`. If file A does `import
B`, and B changes to define a different function that A calls, the scan result for A may change. More directly: `from B import *` or aliases. So to be
fully correct with caching, when B changes, A (which imports B) should be re-scanned. This is what "Unchanged files must return cached results" means:
unchanged files (and files whose dependencies didn't change) can return cached results; if a dependency changed, even if file content unchanged, it
must be re-scanned. Hence dependency tracking with cycle safety.

So design:
- For each scanned file, collect imports (module names) and resolve to files within the scanned files_list. Store in cache entry as `dependencies`
(list of paths).
- When validating a cached file, in addition to checking own hash, check that all dependencies are unchanged (recursively? or just direct?). Typically
direct dependencies suffice: if B changes, re-scan A. If C changes and A->B->C, then B is re-scanned because C changed; A's dependency B changed, so A
re-scanned too. So we need transitive propagation of invalidation.

Implementation:
- Build a reverse dependency graph: for each file F, dependents[F] = list of files that import F.
- When file F is invalidated due to content change (or expiration/config), propagate invalidation to all transitive dependents.
- For circular imports, use a visited set to avoid infinite loops.

Now the cache key:
- Analysis options: tests (-t), skips (-s), severity (-l), confidence (-i).
- Profile name and content.
- Possibly config file content. The requirement says "Profile name and content are part of cache key" and "Analysis options (-t/-s, -l, -i) are part
of cache key". So the cache key should include:
  - profile name (args.profile)
  - profile content (the actual profile dict, e.g. serialized include/exclude/blacklist)
  - tests/skips from CLI (and profile include/exclude?)
  - severity level, confidence level

  Actually -t/-s affect which tests run; -l/-i affect filtering only (not which tests run), but requirement says they're part of cache key. So include
them.

Cache key stored in cache file as `cache_key` hash for the whole run. But different runs may use different keys. If key changed, all entries
invalidate (config_changed). So:
- Each cache file contains a `cache_key` field (hash).
- On load, compute current key; if mismatch => config_changed for all entries.
- If same key, check per-file expiration and hash.

Let me design the cache file structure:

```json
{
  "format_version": 1,
  "cache_dir": "/abs/path",
  "cache_key": "<sha256>",
  "created_at": "...",
  "updated_at": "...",
  "entries": {
    "/abs/path/file.py": {
      "path": "/abs/path/file.py",
      "file_hash": "...",
      "file_size": 123,
      "mtime": 1234567890.0,
      "cached_at": 1234567890.0,
      "dependencies": ["/abs/path/other.py"],
      "skipped": false,
      "skip_reason": null,
      "issues": [ { ...issue dict fields..., "code": ... } ],
      "metrics": { "loc": 4, "nosec": 0, "skipped_tests": 0, ... },
      "score": {"SEVERITY": [..], "CONFIDENCE": [..]}
    }
  }
}
```

Cache key: `sha256` of JSON containing:
- profile name
- profile content (sorted include/exclude, blacklist)
- tests string, skips string
- severity, confidence
- maybe ignore_nosec? The requirement doesn't list it, but it should probably be part of key. Let's include it too; not harmful. Actually adding extra
items to key only invalidates more often, which is safe. But tests might expect a specific key composition. Since we're writing tests too, we can
define. I'll include what's specified plus ignore_nosec, and config content maybe. Actually "Profile name and content are part of cache key" - profile
content already captures tests/skips from profile but CLI -t/-s separately. I'll include:
  - profile_name
  - profile (sorted items)
  - cli_tests
  - cli_skips
  - severity
  - confidence
  - ignore_nosec

Expiration:
- `cache_expiry_days`: if 0 => all entries expired. If >0, entries older than N days invalidated.
- Need to know timestamp stored at cache time.

Cache directory default: `.bandit_cache` (in current working directory). Config can override `cache_directory`. CLI `--cache-dir` overrides.

`--cache-size-limit`: limit on cache size in bytes. When exceeded, prune oldest entries. We can implement by checking total size of cache file(s)
after save and pruning entries until under limit.

CLI operations:
- `--clear-cache`: delete cache directory (if exists) and exit 0.
- `--cache-summary`: load cache and print "Cached files: N", exit 0.
- `--warm-cache`: run scan in incremental mode but don't report results, exit 0.
- `--export-cache FILE`: export cache to JSON file (with format_version), exit 0.
- `--import-cache FILE`: import and merge cache from exported file, exit 0. Graceful on incompatible format/malformed.
- `--list-cached-files`: print one path per line, exit 0.
- `--prune-cache DAYS`: remove entries older than N days, exit 0.
- `--cache-stats`: print stats including cache_file_size_bytes, exit 0.

These cache maintenance operations should work without a target? The task says targets are required (`if not args.targets: sys.exit(2)`). But
--clear-cache etc. probably shouldn't require targets. Let's handle: if a cache maintenance action is specified, allow it without targets.

Let me think about how these interact with the main scan. The operations like --clear-cache, --cache-summary, --list-cached-files, --prune-cache,
--cache-stats, --export-cache, --import-cache could be "standalone" actions. But --warm-cache and --force-rescan are for normal scans. I'll implement:
- `--clear-cache`: standalone (no targets needed).
- `--cache-summary`: standalone.
- `--list-cached-files`: standalone.
- `--prune-cache DAYS`: standalone.
- `--cache-stats`: standalone.
- `--export-cache FILE`: standalone (or after scan?). Could combine with scan? Probably standalone.
- `--import-cache FILE`: standalone.
- `--warm-cache`: normal scan mode but suppress output.
- `--force-rescan`: normal scan mode.

Let me now check how the project's tests are structured and set up a new branch. Then I'll write the code.

class BanditCLIMainLoggerTests(testtools.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.logger = logging.getLogger()
        self.original_logger_handlers = self.logger.handlers
        self.original_logger_level = self.logger.level
        self.logger.handlers = []

    def tearDown(self):
        super().tearDown()
        self.logger.handlers = self.original_logger_handlers
        self.logger.level = self.original_logger_level

    def test_init_logger(self):
        # Test that a logger was properly initialized
        bandit._init_logger()

        self.assertIsNotNone(self.logger)
        self.assertNotEqual(self.logger.handlers, [])
        self.assertEqual(logging.INFO, self.logger.level)

    def test_init_logger_debug_mode(self):
        # Test that the logger's level was set at 'DEBUG'
        bandit._init_logger(logging.DEBUG)
        self.assertEqual(logging.DEBUG, self.logger.level)


class BanditCLIMainTests(testtools.TestCase):
    def setUp(self):
        super().setUp()
        self.current_directory = os.getcwd()

    def tearDown(self):
        super().tearDown()
        os.chdir(self.current_directory)

    def test_get_options_from_ini_no_ini_path_no_target(self):
        # Test that no config options are loaded when no ini path or target
        # directory are provided
        self.assertIsNone(bandit._get_options_from_ini(None, []))

    def test_get_options_from_ini_empty_directory_no_target(self):
        # Test that no config options are loaded when an empty directory is
        # provided as the ini path and no target directory is provided
        ini_directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.assertIsNone(bandit._get_options_from_ini(ini_directory, []))

    def test_get_options_from_ini_no_ini_path_no_bandit_files(self):
        # Test that no config options are loaded when no ini path is provided
        # and the target directory contains no bandit config files (.bandit)
        target_directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        self.assertIsNone(
            bandit._get_options_from_ini(None, [target_directory])
        )

    def test_get_options_from_ini_no_ini_path_multi_bandit_files(self):
        # Test that bandit exits when no ini path is provided and the target
        # directory(s) contain multiple bandit config files (.bandit)
        target_directory = self.useFixture(fixtures.TempDir()).path
        second_config = "second_config_directory"
        os.mkdir(os.path.join(target_directory, second_config))
        bandit_config_one = os.path.join(target_directory, ".bandit")
        bandit_config_two = os.path.join(
            target_directory, second_config, ".bandit"
        )
        bandit_files = [bandit_config_one, bandit_config_two]
        for bandit_file in bandit_files:
            with open(bandit_file, "w") as fd:
                fd.write(bandit_config_content)
        self.assertRaisesRegex(
            SystemExit,
            "2",
            bandit._get_options_from_ini,
            None,
            [target_directory],
        )

    def test_init_extensions(self):
        # Test that an extension loader manager is returned
        self.assertEqual(ext_loader.MANAGER, bandit._init_extensions())

    def test_log_option_source_arg_val(self):
        # Test that the command argument value is returned when provided
        # with None or a string default value
        arg_val = "file"
        ini_val = "vuln"
        option_name = "aggregate"
        for default_val in (None, "default"):
            self.assertEqual(
                arg_val,
                bandit._log_option_source(
                    default_val, arg_val, ini_val, option_name
                ),
            )

    def test_log_option_source_ini_value(self):
        # Test that the ini value is returned when no command argument is
        # provided
        default_val = None
        ini_val = "vuln"
        option_name = "aggregate"
        self.assertEqual(
            ini_val,
            bandit._log_option_source(default_val, None, ini_val, option_name),
        )

    def test_log_option_source_ini_val_with_str_default_and_no_arg_val(self):
        # Test that the ini value is returned when no command argument is
        # provided
        default_val = "file"
        arg_val = "file"
        ini_val = "vuln"
        option_name = "aggregate"
        self.assertEqual(
            ini_val,
            bandit._log_option_source(
                default_val, arg_val, ini_val, option_name
            ),
        )

    def test_log_option_source_no_values(self):
        # Test that None is returned when no command argument or ini value are
        # provided
        option_name = "aggregate"
        self.assertIsNone(
            bandit._log_option_source(None, None, None, option_name)
        )

    @mock.patch("sys.argv", ["bandit", "-c", "bandit.yaml", "test"])

            mock_mgr_pop_bl.side_effect = IOError
            # assert a SystemExit with code 2
            self.assertRaisesRegex(SystemExit, "2", bandit.main)

```python
def _resolve_import(module_name, files_list, current_file_dir):
    parts = module_name.split(".")
    candidates = []
    for i in range(len(parts), 0, -1):
        base = os.path.join(*parts[:i])
        suffix = parts[i:]
        if suffix:
            # module.submodule -> module/submodule.py or module/submodule/__init__.py
            pass
    # simpler: module_name.replace(".", "/") + ".py", and __init__.py
    rel = module_name.replace(".", "/")
    for f in files_list:
        f_abs = os.path.abspath(f)
        if f_abs.endswith(rel + ".py") or f_abs.endswith(rel + "/__init__.py"):
            return f_abs
    return None
```

### Cache key computation

Where to compute cache key? In CLI after profile is built. The profile content includes sets (include/exclude) and possibly blacklist. We need to
serialize to JSON. Sets are not JSON serializable, so convert to sorted lists.

```python
def _make_cache_key(profile, args, ignore_nosec):
    profile_data = {
        "profile_name": args.profile,
        "profile_include": sorted(profile.get("include", [])),
        "profile_exclude": sorted(profile.get("exclude", [])),
        "profile_blacklist": profile.get("blacklist", {}),
        "cli_tests": args.tests,
        "cli_skips": args.skips,
        "severity": args.severity,
        "confidence": args.confidence,
        "ignore_nosec": ignore_nosec,
    }
    return hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(profile_data, sort_keys=True, default=str).encode()).hexdigest()
```

```python
def _cache_hash_default(obj):
    if isinstance(obj, set):
        return sorted(obj)
    if isinstance(obj, (bytes,)):
        return obj.decode("utf-8", "replace")
    return str(obj)
```

### Cache file structure & integrity

I'll implement `BanditCache` in `bandit/core/cache.py`:

```python
import hashlib, json, logging, os, time, shutil

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class BanditCache:
    FORMAT_VERSION = 1
    CACHE_FILENAME = "cache.json"

    def __init__(self, cache_dir, cache_key, expiry_days=30, size_limit=None):
        self.cache_dir = os.path.abspath(cache_dir)
        self.cache_path = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, self.CACHE_FILENAME)
        self.cache_key = cache_key
        self.expiry_days = expiry_days
        self.size_limit = size_limit
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = False
        self._load()

    def _load(self):
        """Load cache from disk, validating integrity. Corrupted entries discarded."""
        if not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
            return
        try:
            with open(self.cache_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                data = json.load(f)
            if not isinstance(data, dict):
                raise ValueError("cache root is not a dict")
            if data.get("format_version") != self.FORMAT_VERSION:
                LOG.warning("Cache format version mismatch; ignoring cache")
                return
            self.stored_cache_key = data.get("cache_key")
            entries = data.get("entries", {})
            if not isinstance(entries, dict):
                raise ValueError("entries not a dict")
            for path, entry in entries.items():
                if self._valid_entry(entry):
                    self.entries[path] = entry
                else:
                    LOG.debug("Discarding corrupted cache entry for %s", path)
                    self.dirty = True
        except (OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError) as e:
            LOG.warning("Cache file is corrupted, ignoring: %s", e)
            self.entries = {}
            self.dirty = True
```

Wait, if `format_version` mismatch, the whole cache should be discarded. The requirement: "Cache must validate integrity on load and discard corrupted
entries." For format version mismatch, we should probably discard the whole cache (not just entries). We'll return empty entries. But also, the
`--import-cache` says "incompatible format_version or malformed input is discarded gracefully". So in load, incompatible format_version => discard
whole cache.

`_valid_entry(entry)`:
```python
def _valid_entry(self, entry):
    return (
        isinstance(entry, dict)
        and isinstance(entry.get("path"), str)
        and isinstance(entry.get("file_hash"), str)
        and isinstance(entry.get("issues"), list)
    )
```

Also store `cached_at` etc. If missing some fields, discard entry.

### Cache lookup logic

I'll implement in manager or in cache class? Let me put the validation logic in manager because it needs file access and dependency graph. But cache
class can hold the data. Let me define:

In manager:
```python
def _prepare_cache(self, cache, force_rescan):
    """Decide which files to scan and load cached results."""
    files_to_scan = []
    valid_paths = set(cache.entries.keys())
    # Build canonical map
    canonical = {os.path.abspath(f): f for f in self.files_list}

    # First pass: check each file's own validity
    status = {}
    for fname in self.files_list:
        if fname == "-":
            status[fname] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        path = os.path.abspath(fname)
        if force_rescan:
            status[fname] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        entry = cache.entries.get(path)
        if entry is None:
            status[fname] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        if cache.stored_cache_key != cache.cache_key:
            status[fname] = "config_changed"
            ...
        if self._cache_entry_expired(entry, cache.expiry_days):
            status[fname] = "expired"
            ...
        if not self._file_unchanged(path, entry):
            status[fname] = "file_changed"
            ...
        # valid
        status[fname] = "valid"

    # Second pass: dependency propagation. For files marked file_changed, invalidate dependents transitively.
    # Build dependency graph from cache entries.
    dependents = collections.defaultdict(list)
    for path, entry in cache.entries.items():
        for dep in entry.get("dependencies", []):
            dependents[dep].append(path)

    invalid = set()
    def propagate(fpath):
        if fpath in invalid: return
        invalid.add(fpath)
        for dependent in dependents.get(fpath, []):
            if dependent in cache.entries:
                propagate(dependent)

    for fname, st in status.items():
        if st in ("file_changed", "config_changed", "expired", "not_cached"):
            if fname != "-":
                propagate(os.path.abspath(fname))

    # Now assign final statuses
    for fname in self.files_list:
        path = os.path.abspath(fname)
        if force_rescan or path in invalid or status[fname] != "valid":
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            reason = status[fname]
            if path in invalid and status[fname] == "valid":
                reason = "file_changed"  # dependency changed
            self._record_cache_miss(reason)
        else:
            self._load_cached_file(fname, path, cache.entries[path])
    return files_to_scan
```

Wait, but the status dict uses fname keys while propagation uses path. Need consistent mapping. Let me simplify by using path as the key in status and
a map from path -> fname.

Let me write carefully.

Also, after scan, store results. Let me add a method `_save_cache_entries(cache)` that iterates over scanned files and stores entries. The scanned
files are those that went through the loop. But some may have been skipped (syntax error). For skipped files, we should store an entry with `skipped:

### Dependencies

Need to compute imports for scanned files. I'll add a function in manager that parses AST import statements:

```python
def _get_imports(fname, data):
    imports = set()
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(data)
        for node in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
                for alias in node.names:
                    imports.add(alias.name)
            elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
                if node.module:
                    imports.add(node.module)
    except SyntaxError:
        pass
    return imports
```

Then resolve each import to a file path in files_list:
```python
def _resolve_import_dependencies(module_names, files_list):
    deps = set()
    for mod in module_names:
        rel = mod.replace(".", "/")
        for f in files_list:
            f_abs = os.path.abspath(f)
            if f_abs.endswith("/" + rel + ".py") or f_abs.endswith("/" + rel + "/__init__.py"):
                deps.add(f_abs)
    return deps
```

This handles circular imports naturally: A imports B, B imports A -> deps are symmetric; propagation uses visited set.

But wait: should dependencies be only files in files_list? Yes, because only those are cached. Imports of external packages are not in cache, so no
dependency tracking.

The task explicitly says "Circular imports must not cause infinite loops." Our propagation with visited set handles that.

r"""

Text Formatter

This formatter outputs the issues as plain text.

def get_verbose_details(manager):
    bits = []
    bits.append(f"Files in scope ({len(manager.files_list)}):")
    tpl = "\t%s (score: {SEVERITY: %i, CONFIDENCE: %i})"
    bits.extend(
        [
            tpl % (item, sum(score["SEVERITY"]), sum(score["CONFIDENCE"]))
            for (item, score) in zip(manager.files_list, manager.scores)
        ]
    )
    bits.append(f"Files excluded ({len(manager.excluded_files)}):")
    bits.extend([f"\t{fname}" for fname in manager.excluded_files])
    return "\n".join([bit for bit in bits])


def get_metrics(manager):
    bits = []
    bits.append("\nRun metrics:")
    for criteria, _ in constants.CRITERIA:
        bits.append(f"\tTotal issues (by {criteria.lower()}):")
        for rank in constants.RANKING:
            bits.append(
                "\t\t%s: %s"
                % (
                    rank.capitalize(),
                    manager.metrics.data["_totals"][f"{criteria}.{rank}"],
                )
            )
    return "\n".join([bit for bit in bits])


def _output_issue_str(
    issue, indent, show_lineno=True, show_code=True, lines=-1
):
    # returns a list of lines that should be added to the existing lines list
    bits = []
    bits.append(
        f"{indent}>> Issue: [{issue.test_id}:{issue.test}] {issue.text}"
    )

    bits.append(
        "%s   Severity: %s   Confidence: %s"
        % (
            indent,
            issue.severity.capitalize(),
            issue.confidence.capitalize(),
        )
    )

    bits.append(f"{indent}   CWE: {str(issue.cwe)}")

    bits.append(f"{indent}   More Info: {docs_utils.get_url(issue.test_id)}")

    bits.append(
        "%s   Location: %s:%s:%s"
        % (
            indent,
            issue.fname,
            issue.lineno if show_lineno else "",
            issue.col_offset if show_lineno else "",
        )
    )

    if show_code:
        bits.extend(
            [indent + line for line in issue.get_code(lines, True).split("\n")]
        )

    return "\n".join([bit for bit in bits])


def get_results(manager, sev_level, conf_level, lines):
    bits = []
    issues = manager.get_issue_list(sev_level, conf_level)
    baseline = not isinstance(issues, list)
    candidate_indent = " " * 10

    if not len(issues):
        return "\tNo issues identified."

    for issue in issues:
        # if not a baseline or only one candidate we know the issue
        if not baseline or len(issues[issue]) == 1:
            bits.append(_output_issue_str(issue, "", lines=lines))

        # otherwise show the finding and the candidates
        else:
            bits.append(
                _output_issue_str(
                    issue, "", show_lineno=False, show_code=False
                )
            )

            bits.append("\n-- Candidate Issues --")
            for candidate in issues[issue]:
                bits.append(
                    _output_issue_str(candidate, candidate_indent, lines=lines)
                )
                bits.append("\n")
        bits.append("-" * 50)
    return "\n".join([bit for bit in bits])


@test_properties.accepts_baseline
def report(manager, fileobj, sev_level, conf_level, lines=-1):
    """Prints discovered issues in the text format

    :param manager: the bandit manager object
    :param fileobj: The output file object, which may be sys.stdout
    :param sev_level: Filtering severity level
    :param conf_level: Filtering confidence level
    :param lines: Number of lines to report, -1 for all
    """

    bits = []

    if not manager.quiet or manager.results_count(sev_level, conf_level):
        bits.append(
            f"Run started:{datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc)}"
        )

        if manager.verbose:
            bits.append(get_verbose_details(manager))

        bits.append("\nTest results:")
        bits.append(get_results(manager, sev_level, conf_level, lines))
        bits.append("\nCode scanned:")
        bits.append(
            "\tTotal lines of code: %i"
            % (manager.metrics.data["_totals"]["loc"])
        )

        bits.append(
            "\tTotal lines skipped (#nosec): %i"
            % (manager.metrics.data["_totals"]["nosec"])
        )
        bits.append(
            "\tTotal potential issues skipped due to specifically being "
            "disabled (e.g., #nosec BXXX): %i"
            % (manager.metrics.data["_totals"]["skipped_tests"])
        )

        skipped = manager.get_skipped()
        bits.append(get_metrics(manager))
        bits.append(f"Files skipped ({len(skipped)}):")
        bits.extend(["\t%s (%s)" % skip for skip in skipped])
        result = "\n".join([bit for bit in bits]) + "\n"

        with fileobj:
            wrapped_file = utils.wrap_file_object(fileobj)
            wrapped_file.write(result)

    if fileobj.name != sys.stdout.name:
        LOG.info("Text output written to file: %s", fileobj.name)

```python
def _score_from_issues(issues):
    score = {
        "SEVERITY": [0]*4,
        "CONFIDENCE": [0]*4,
    }
    for i in issues:
        sev_idx = constants.RANKING.index(i.severity)
        conf_idx = constants.RANKING.index(i.confidence)
        score["SEVERITY"][sev_idx] += constants.RANKING_VALUES[i.severity]
        score["CONFIDENCE"][conf_idx] += constants.RANKING_VALUES[i.confidence]
    return score
```

So the flow:

```python
def run_tests(self, cache=None, force_rescan=False):
    files_to_scan = self.files_list
    if cache is not None:
        files_to_scan = self._prepare_cache(cache, force_rescan)

    new_files_list = list(self.files_list)
    scan_scores = {}
    scan_info = {}  # fname -> {score, skipped, skip_reason}

    for fname in files_to_scan:
        try:
            if fname == "-": ...
            else: with open...
                self._parse_file(fname, fdata, new_files_list, scan_scores)
        except OSError as e:
            self.skipped.append((fname, e.strerror))
            new_files_list.remove(fname)

    self.files_list = new_files_list

    if cache is not None:
        cache.store_scanned(self, scan_info)   # or handled inside
        ...

    # Build scores in files_list order
    self.scores = []
    for fname in self.files_list:
        if fname in cached_scores:
            self.scores.append(cached_scores[fname])
        elif fname in scan_scores:
            self.scores.append(scan_scores[fname])

    self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_hits"] = self.cache_hits
    self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_misses"] = self.cache_misses
    self.metrics.aggregate()
```

```python
class BanditCache:
```

### CLI arguments

Add to parser:
```python
parser.add_argument("--incremental", dest="incremental", action="store_true", default=None, help="enable incremental analysis using cache")
parser.add_argument("--no-incremental", dest="incremental", action="store_false", help="disable incremental analysis (default)")
parser.add_argument("--cache-dir", dest="cache_dir", action="store", default=None, help="directory to store cache (default: .bandit_cache)")
parser.add_argument("--cache-size-limit", dest="cache_size_limit", action="store", default=None, type=int, help="maximum cache size in bytes")
parser.add_argument("--clear-cache", dest="clear_cache", action="store_true", default=False, help="clear the cache directory and exit")
parser.add_argument("--cache-summary", dest="cache_summary", action="store_true", default=False, help="print number of cached files and exit")
parser.add_argument("--force-rescan", dest="force_rescan", action="store_true", default=False, help="force rescan of all files, ignoring cache")
parser.add_argument("--warm-cache", dest="warm_cache", action="store_true", default=False, help="pre-populate cache without reporting issues")
parser.add_argument("--export-cache", dest="export_cache", action="store", default=None, metavar="FILE", help="export cache to a JSON file and exit")
parser.add_argument("--import-cache", dest="import_cache", action="store", default=None, metavar="FILE", help="import and merge cache from a JSON file
and exit")
parser.add_argument("--list-cached-files", dest="list_cached_files", action="store_true", default=False, help="list files in cache, one per line")
parser.add_argument("--prune-cache", dest="prune_cache", action="store", default=None, metavar="DAYS", type=int, help="remove cached entries older
than DAYS days")
parser.add_argument("--cache-stats", dest="cache_stats", action="store_true", default=False, help="print cache statistics")
```

Default incremental from config: `incremental_analysis.enabled`. So after loading config, set `args.incremental` if it's None and config has enabled
True.

But note: argparse with `action="store_true", default=None` works: if `--no-incremental` passed, sets False; if neither, remains None; then fall back
to config.

### Standalone cache operations

These operations don't need targets. I'll handle them after config load and before profile setup maybe, because they need cache config. The cache dir
config comes from config file (`incremental_analysis.cache_directory`). So load config first, then handle standalone ops.

Let me think about the flow in `main()`:

1. Parse args.
2. Load config (`b_conf`).
3. If no targets and no cache maintenance op -> print usage and exit 2.
4. Compute incremental settings:
   - enabled = args.incremental if args.incremental is not None else config.get_option("incremental_analysis.enabled") or False
   - cache_dir = args.cache_dir or config.get_option("incremental_analysis.cache_directory") or ".bandit_cache"
   - expiry_days = config.get_option("incremental_analysis.cache_expiry_days") or 30 (if 0, expires all)
   - size_limit = args.cache_size_limit
5. Handle standalone ops:
   - `--clear-cache`: if dir missing -> no-op exit 0; else shutil.rmtree; exit 0.
   - `--cache-summary`: load cache, print "Cached files: N", exit 0.
   - `--list-cached-files`: load cache, print each path, exit 0.
   - `--prune-cache DAYS`: load cache, remove entries older than DAYS, save, exit 0.
   - `--cache-stats`: load cache, print stats, exit 0.
   - `--export-cache FILE`: load cache, write to file, exit 0.
   - `--import-cache FILE`: load exported file, merge into cache, save, exit 0.

Wait — for --clear-cache, we don't need to load cache; just remove dir.

For these operations, do we need `profile`? No. So I should handle them before the profile/b_manager code. But `_get_profile` needs args.profile etc.
and requires targets? Actually `_get_profile` doesn't need targets. But `b_conf` load might fail for missing config. That's fine.

But there's a question: do we still require targets for normal scan? Yes. So:

```python
cache_actions = args.clear_cache or args.cache_summary or args.list_cached_files or args.prune_cache is not None or args.cache_stats or
args.export_cache is not None or args.import_cache is not None
if not args.targets and not cache_actions:
    parser.print_usage()
    sys.exit(2)
```

Then after b_conf load and cache settings, handle cache actions and return.

For `--warm-cache`: it implies incremental. It should still require targets and run scan. After run_tests, exit 0 without outputting results.

### Cache directory auto-creation

`BanditCache` should `os.makedirs(cache_dir, exist_ok=True)` on init or save.

### Cache size limit

After saving, if `size_limit` and total size > limit, remove oldest entries (by cached_at) until under limit, then save again. Implement in `save()`.

### Export/import

Export: write `{"format_version": FORMAT_VERSION, "cache_key": ..., "entries": {...}}` to FILE. Include `format_version`.

Import: read file; if `format_version != FORMAT_VERSION` or malformed -> discard gracefully (exit 0). If valid, merge entries into current cache
(update/add), save.

Note: when importing, should we also import the cache_key? If we import entries from a different cache key, they'd be invalidated on next lookup due
to key mismatch. But "import and merge cache from a previously exported file" - presumably it imports the cache data for same key. We should probably
keep the current cache's key (or set to imported key?). Hmm. If importing into a fresh cache dir, the imported cache_key should be used, otherwise all
imported entries invalidate. Let me set `self.cache_key = data["cache_key"]` on import? But then subsequent scans with a different key would
invalidate all - which is correct. However, if the current cache has existing entries with current key and we import entries with a different key,
mixing keys in one file is problematic because lookup compares one stored key.

Better design: store `cache_key` per entry, not globally. Then each entry has its own key. Lookup compares entry's key to current key. That's more
flexible for import merging. But the spec says "Profile name and content are part of cache key", not necessarily a single global key. Storing
per-entry key is cleaner.

Let me redesign:
- Each entry has `"cache_key"`.
- `stored_cache_key` global is not needed; we can still store a global key for reference.
- On lookup: if entry["cache_key"] != current key -> config_changed invalidation.
- On import: merge entries with their own keys.
- On save: entries preserved.

But then the `cache_key` in exported file is per-entry. Fine.

Actually the spec says cache_info has `invalidation_counts.config_changed`. This covers both config changes and profile/options changes (key
mismatch). Good.

Let me store per-entry key. And also a top-level `cache_key` for reference / export. On import, we can leave top-level key as-is; entries carry their
own.

### Cache validation & corrupted entries

- On `_load`, validate each entry with required fields; discard invalid ones.
- On lookup, if entry missing required fields, treat as not_cached (or discard).

### Determining expiry

`cache_expiry_days=0` expires all entries. If `expiry_days > 0`: entry is expired if `(now - entry["cached_at"]) > expiry_days * 86400`. Since 0
expires all, simply: `if expiry_days == 0: expired`. Or `if entry["cached_at"] < now - expiry_days*86400` which for 0 means all entries with cached_at
< now (all) are expired. Equivalent. But `cached_at` is epoch float; for 0 days, any cached_at in the past is expired. Good.

### File changed detection

Use file content hash (sha256). Compute hash of file bytes. Compare with entry["file_hash"]. Also can use size/mtime but hash is definitive. To handle
`<stdin>`, skip caching.

### JSON cache_info

Add `machine_output["cache_info"] = {...}` always.

### Metrics cache_hits/cache_misses

Add to `Metrics.__init__`:
```python
self.data["_totals"]["cache_hits"] = 0
self.data["_totals"]["cache_misses"] = 0
```

    @mock.patch("bandit.core.issue.Issue.get_code")
    def test_output_issue(self, get_code):
        issue = _get_issue_instance()
        get_code.return_value = "DDDDDDD"
        indent_val = "CCCCCCC"

        def _template(_issue, _indent_val, _code):
            return_val = [
                "{}>> Issue: [{}:{}] {}".format(
                    _indent_val, _issue.test_id, _issue.test, _issue.text
                ),
                "{}   Severity: {}   Confidence: {}".format(

                "{}   Location: {}:{}:{}".format(
                    _indent_val, _issue.fname, _issue.lineno, _issue.col_offset
                ),

            if _code:
                return_val.append(f"{_indent_val}{_code}")
            return "\n".join(return_val)

        issue_text = b_text._output_issue_str(issue, indent_val)

            issue, indent_val, show_code=False

        issue.col_offset = ""
        issue_text = b_text._output_issue_str(
            issue, indent_val, show_lineno=False
        )
        expected_return = _template(issue, indent_val, "DDDDDDD")

Let me now write `bandit/core/cache.py`. I'll include:

```python
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import time

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

CACHE_FILENAME = "cache.json"
FORMAT_VERSION = 1


def _hash_file(path):
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(65536), b""):
            h.update(chunk)
    return h.hexdigest()


def cache_key_from_parts(**parts):
    data = json.dumps(parts, sort_keys=True, default=_json_default)
    return hashlib.sha256(data.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def _json_default(obj):
    if isinstance(obj, set):
        return sorted(obj)
    if isinstance(obj, bytes):
        return obj.decode("utf-8", "replace")
    return str(obj)


class BanditCache:
    def __init__(self, cache_dir, cache_key=None, expiry_days=30, size_limit=None):
        self.cache_dir = os.path.abspath(cache_dir)
        self.cache_path = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, CACHE_FILENAME)
        self.cache_key = cache_key
        self.expiry_days = expiry_days
        self.size_limit = size_limit
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = False
        self._load()

    def _load(self):
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = False
        if not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
            return
        try:
            with open(self.cache_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                data = json.load(f)
            if not isinstance(data, dict):
                raise ValueError("cache root is not a dictionary")
            if data.get("format_version") != FORMAT_VERSION:
                LOG.warning("Cache has incompatible format version, ignoring")
                self.dirty = True
                return
            entries = data.get("entries")
            if not isinstance(entries, dict):
                raise ValueError("cache entries are not a dictionary")
            for path, entry in entries.items():
                if self._valid_entry(entry):
                    self.entries[path] = entry
                else:
                    LOG.debug("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", path)
                    self.dirty = True
        except (OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError) as e:
            LOG.warning("Cache is corrupted and will be discarded: %s", e)
            self.entries = {}
            self.dirty = True
```

Wait, `json.JSONDecodeError` is a subclass of ValueError, so I can just catch `(OSError, ValueError)`.

`_valid_entry`:
```python
def _valid_entry(self, entry):
    return (
        isinstance(entry, dict)
        and isinstance(entry.get("path"), str)
        and isinstance(entry.get("file_hash"), str)
        and isinstance(entry.get("issues"), list)
        and isinstance(entry.get("metrics"), dict)
        and isinstance(entry.get("cached_at"), (int, float))
        and "cache_key" in entry
    )
```

Store/access methods:
```python
def get(self, path):
    return self.entries.get(path)

def __contains__(self, path): return path in self.entries

def store(self, path, entry):
    self.entries[path] = entry
    self.dirty = True

def remove(self, path):
    if path in self.entries:
        del self.entries[path]
        self.dirty = True

def clear(self):
    self.entries = {}
    self.dirty = True

def save(self):
    if not self.entries and not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
        # nothing to save and no existing cache
        return False
    os.makedirs(self.cache_dir, exist_ok=True)
    data = {
        "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
        "cache_key": self.cache_key,
        "created_at": ...,
        "updated_at": time.time(),
        "entries": self.entries,
    }
    tmp_path = self.cache_path + ".tmp"
    with open(tmp_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        json.dump(data, f, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
    os.replace(tmp_path, self.cache_path)
    self.dirty = False
    self._enforce_size_limit()
    return True
```

But `save()` should not drop entries when cache_key is None (e.g., standalone import). Fine.

`_enforce_size_limit`:
```python
def _enforce_size_limit(self):
    if not self.size_limit:
        return
    if not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
        return
    size = os.path.getsize(self.cache_path)
    if size <= self.size_limit:
        return
    # remove oldest entries until under limit
    while self.entries and size > self.size_limit:
        oldest_path = min(self.entries, key=lambda p: self.entries[p].get("cached_at", 0))
        del self.entries[oldest_path]
        # rewrite
        data = {...}
        tmp...
        os.replace...
        size = os.path.getsize(self.cache_path)
```

`clear_cache()` static method:
```python
@staticmethod
def clear(cache_dir):
    if os.path.isdir(cache_dir):
        shutil.rmtree(cache_dir)
```

`export(fileobj)`:
```python
def export(self, fileobj):
    data = {
        "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
        "cache_key": self.cache_key,
        "exported_at": time.time(),
        "entries": self.entries,
    }
    json.dump(data, fileobj, indent=2, sort_keys=True)
```

`import_file(path)`:
```python
def import_cache(self, path):
    try:
        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            data = json.load(f)
        if not isinstance(data, dict) or data.get("format_version") != FORMAT_VERSION:
            LOG.warning("Ignoring incompatible cache import: %s", path)
            return
        entries = data.get("entries")
        if not isinstance(entries, dict):
            raise ValueError("entries not dict")
        for p, e in entries.items():
            if self._valid_entry(e):
                self.entries[p] = e
                self.dirty = True
            else:
                LOG.warning("Ignoring invalid entry %s from import", p)
        self.save()
    except (OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError) as e:
        LOG.warning("Ignoring malformed cache import %s: %s", path, e)
```

`list_files()`: return sorted paths.
`prune(days)`:
```python
def prune(self, days):
    if days < 0:
        return 0
    cutoff = time.time() - days * 86400
    removed = 0
    for path in list(self.entries):
        if self.entries[path].get("cached_at", 0) < cutoff:
            del self.entries[path]
            removed += 1
            self.dirty = True
    if removed:
        self.save()
    return removed
```

`stats()`:
```python
def stats(self):
    size = os.path.getsize(self.cache_path) if os.path.exists(self.cache_path) else 0
    return {
        "cache_dir": self.cache_dir,
        "cache_file_size_bytes": size,
        "cached_files": len(self.entries),
        "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
    }
```

```python
def run_tests(self, cache=None, force_rescan=False):
    self.cache = cache
    cached_scores = {}
    scan_scores = {}
    scan_cache_data = {}
    files_to_scan = self.files_list
    if cache is not None:
        files_to_scan, cached_scores = self._prepare_cache(cache, force_rescan)

    new_files_list = list(self.files_list)
    if len(files_to_scan) > PROGRESS_THRESHOLD ...:
        files = progress.track(files_to_scan)
    else:
        files = files_to_scan

    for fname in files:
        LOG.debug(...)
        try:
            if fname == "-":
                open_fd = os.fdopen(sys.stdin.fileno(), "rb", 0)
                fdata = io.BytesIO(open_fd.read())
                new_files_list = ["<stdin>" if x == "-" else x for x in new_files_list]
                self._parse_file("<stdin>", fdata, new_files_list, scan_scores, scan_cache_data)
            else:
                with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
                    self._parse_file(fname, fdata, new_files_list, scan_scores, scan_cache_data)
        except OSError as e:
            self.skipped.append((fname, e.strerror))
            new_files_list.remove(fname)

    self.files_list = new_files_list

    if cache is not None:
        self._store_cache_entries(cache, scan_cache_data)
        cache.save()

    # Build scores aligned with files_list
    self.scores = []
    for fname in self.files_list:
        if fname in cached_scores:
            self.scores.append(cached_scores[fname])
        elif fname in scan_scores:
            self.scores.append(scan_scores[fname])
        else:
            # shouldn't happen, but add zero score
            self.scores.append(self._empty_score())

    self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_hits"] = self.cache_hits
    self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_misses"] = self.cache_misses
    self.metrics.aggregate()
```

Now `_prepare_cache(cache, force_rescan)`:

```python
def _prepare_cache(self, cache, force_rescan):
    files_to_scan = []
    cached_scores = {}
    # map path -> fname
    path_to_fname = {}
    fname_to_path = {}
    for fname in self.files_list:
        path = os.path.abspath(fname)
        path_to_fname[path] = fname
        fname_to_path[fname] = path

    # Determine per-file status
    statuses = {}  # path -> reason string or "valid"
    for fname in self.files_list:
        path = fname_to_path[fname]
        if fname == "<stdin>" or fname == "-":
            statuses[path] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        if force_rescan:
            statuses[path] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        entry = cache.get(path)
        if entry is None:
            statuses[path] = "not_cached"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        if entry.get("cache_key") != cache.cache_key:
            statuses[path] = "config_changed"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        if self._cache_entry_expired(entry, cache.expiry_days):
            statuses[path] = "expired"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        if not self._file_unchanged(path, entry):
            statuses[path] = "file_changed"
            files_to_scan.append(fname)
            continue
        statuses[path] = "valid"

    # Build reverse dependency graph and propagate invalidation
    invalid_paths = set()
    dependents = collections.defaultdict(list)
    for path, entry in cache.entries.items():
        for dep in entry.get("dependencies", []) or []:
            dependents[dep].append(path)

    def _invalidate(path):
        if path in invalid_paths:
            return
        invalid_paths.add(path)
        for dep_path in dependents.get(path, []):
            _invalidate(dep_path)

    for path, status in statuses.items():
        if status != "valid":
            _invalidate(path)

    # Now finalize
    for fname in self.files_list:
        path = fname_to_path[fname]
        if fname in ("-", "<stdin>"):
            continue  # already added to files_to_scan
        if path in invalid_paths:
            if statuses[path] == "valid":
                statuses[path] = "file_changed"  # dependency changed
            if fname not in files_to_scan:
                files_to_scan.append(fname)
            self._record_cache_miss(statuses[path])
            continue
        if statuses[path] != "valid":
            # already added in first pass
            continue
        # valid cache hit
        self._load_cached_file(fname, path, cache.get(path), cached_scores)
    return files_to_scan, cached_scores
```

Hmm, there's a subtle issue: in the first pass I add files_to_scan for each invalid status. Then in propagation, some valid files become invalid
(dependency changed). I add them too. But a file might be added twice; use a set or check `if fname not in files_to_scan`. Also, for
`not_cached`/`config_changed`/`expired`/`file_changed`, I need to call `_record_cache_miss` once. Let me restructure to avoid double-counting:

Simpler:
```python
for fname in self.files_list:
    path = fname_to_path[fname]
    if fname in ("-", "<stdin>"):
        files_to_scan.append(fname)
        self._record_cache_miss("not_cached")
        continue
    status = statuses[path]
    if status == "valid" and path not in invalid_paths:
        self._load_cached_file(...)
    else:
        reason = status if status != "valid" else "file_changed"
        files_to_scan.append(fname)
        self._record_cache_miss(reason)
```

And the first pass doesn't append files_to_scan; it only computes statuses. Then this final loop decides. That's cleaner.

`_record_cache_miss(reason)`:
```python
def _record_cache_miss(self, reason):
    self.cache_misses += 1
    if reason in self.invalidation_counts:
        self.invalidation_counts[reason] += 1
    else:
        self.invalidation_counts["not_cached"] += 1
```

`_cache_entry_expired(entry, expiry_days)`:
```python
def _cache_entry_expired(self, entry, expiry_days):
    if expiry_days == 0:
        return True
    cached_at = entry.get("cached_at", 0)
    return time.time() - cached_at > expiry_days * 86400
```

`_file_unchanged(path, entry)`:
```python
def _file_unchanged(self, path, entry):
    try:
        current_hash = b_cache.hash_file(path)
    except OSError:
        return False
    return current_hash == entry.get("file_hash")
```

Actually put hash_file in cache module as a module-level function `file_hash(path)`.

```python
def _load_cached_file(self, fname, path, entry, cached_scores):
    self.files_cached.append(fname)
    self.cache_hits += 1
    # restore issues
    for issue_dict in entry.get("issues", []):
        try:
            self.results.append(issue.issue_from_dict(issue_dict))
        except Exception:
            LOG.debug("Discarding invalid cached issue for %s", fname)
    # restore metrics
    metrics_data = entry.get("metrics")
    if isinstance(metrics_data, dict):
        self.metrics.data[fname] = metrics_data
    # restore skip status
    if entry.get("skipped"):
        self.skipped.append((fname, entry.get("skip_reason") or "cached skipped file"))
        # but note: skipped files should be removed from files_list?
```

`_score_from_issues(issue_dicts)`:
```python
def _score_from_issues(self, issue_dicts):
    score = {
        "SEVERITY": [0] * len(constants.RANKING),
        "CONFIDENCE": [0] * len(constants.RANKING),
    }
    for idict in issue_dicts:
        try:
            sev = idict["issue_severity"]
            conf = idict["issue_confidence"]
            sev_idx = constants.RANKING.index(sev)
            conf_idx = constants.RANKING.index(conf)
            score["SEVERITY"][sev_idx] += constants.RANKING_VALUES[sev]
            score["CONFIDENCE"][conf_idx] += constants.RANKING_VALUES[conf]
        except (KeyError, ValueError):
            continue
    return score
```

`_store_cache_entries(cache, scan_cache_data)`:
```python
def _store_cache_entries(self, cache, scan_cache_data):
    # group issues by fname
    issues_by_file = collections.defaultdict(list)
    for res in self.results:
        issues_by_file[res.fname].append(res)

    for fname, info in scan_cache_data.items():
        path = os.path.abspath(fname)
        try:
            fdata = open(fname, "rb").read()  # but for stdin?
        except OSError:
            continue
        file_hash = b_cache.hash_bytes(fdata)
        deps = self._resolve_import_dependencies(fname, fdata, self.files_list + self.files_scanned)
        entry = {
            "path": path,
            "cache_key": cache.cache_key,
            "file_hash": file_hash,
            "file_size": len(fdata),
            "mtime": os.path.getmtime(fname),
            "cached_at": time.time(),
            "dependencies": sorted(deps),
            "skipped": info.get("skipped", False),
            "skip_reason": info.get("skip_reason"),
            "issues": [i.as_dict(with_code=True) for i in issues_by_file.get(fname, [])],
            "metrics": self.metrics.data.get(fname, {}),
        }
        cache.store(path, entry)
```

Let me write `_resolve_import_dependencies`:
```python
def _resolve_import_dependencies(self, fname, fdata, known_paths):
    imports = set()
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(fdata.decode("utf-8", "replace"))
        for node in ast.walk(tree):
            if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
                imports.update(a.name for a in node.names)
            elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom) and node.module:
                imports.add(node.module)
    except SyntaxError:
        pass
    deps = set()
    for mod in imports:
        rel = mod.replace(".", "/")
        for known in known_paths:
            if known.endswith("/" + rel + ".py") or known.endswith("/" + rel + "/__init__.py"):
                deps.add(known)
                break
    return deps
```

`known_paths` should be absolute paths. Let me pass `known_abs_paths`.

Now, one subtlety: when storing a scanned file that imports another scanned file, the dependency path must match the cache key used for that file.
Both use `os.path.abspath(fname)`. Good.

cat > bandit/core/cache.py <<'EOF'
#
# Copyright 2025
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
"""Incremental analysis cache for Bandit.

This module provides a persistent on-disk cache used by Bandit's
``--incremental`` mode.  Results for unchanged files (and files whose
dependencies have not changed) are stored so that they can be returned on
subsequent runs without rescanning the file.
"""
import hashlib
import json
import logging
import os
import shutil
import time

LOG = logging.getLogger(__name__)

CACHE_FILENAME = "cache.json"
FORMAT_VERSION = 1


def _json_default(obj):
    """JSON serializer for objects not natively serializable."""
    if isinstance(obj, set):
        return sorted(obj)
    if isinstance(obj, bytes):
        return obj.decode("utf-8", "replace")
    return str(obj)


def cache_key_from_parts(**parts):
    """Build a stable cache key from analysis-affecting parts.

    :param parts: keyword arguments describing analysis options/profile
    :return: hex digest identifying the analysis configuration
    """
    data = json.dumps(
        parts, sort_keys=True, default=_json_default, separators=(",", ":")
    )
    return hashlib.sha256(data.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()


def file_hash(path):
    """Return a SHA-256 hash of the file at ``path``."""
    hasher = hashlib.sha256()
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(65536), b""):
            hasher.update(chunk)
    return hasher.hexdigest()


def hash_bytes(data):
    """Return a SHA-256 hash of the given bytes."""
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


class BanditCache:
    """A persistent cache for incremental analysis."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        cache_dir,
        cache_key=None,
        expiry_days=30,
        size_limit=None,
    ):
        """Initialize the cache.

        :param cache_dir: Directory where cache data is stored
        :param cache_key: Key identifying the current analysis configuration
        :param expiry_days: Number of days before an entry expires; ``0``
            expires every entry
        :param size_limit: Optional maximum cache file size in bytes
        """
        self.cache_dir = os.path.abspath(cache_dir)
        self.cache_path = os.path.join(self.cache_dir, CACHE_FILENAME)
        self.cache_key = cache_key
        self.expiry_days = expiry_days
        self.size_limit = size_limit
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = False
        self._load()

    def _load(self):
        """Load cache from disk, validating integrity.

        Corrupted entries are discarded.  A cache file with an incompatible
        format version is ignored entirely.
        """
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = False
        if not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
            return

        try:
            with open(self.cache_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                data = json.load(f)
            if not isinstance(data, dict):
                raise ValueError("cache root is not a dictionary")
            if data.get("format_version") != FORMAT_VERSION:
                LOG.warning(
                    "Cache has incompatible format version, ignoring cache"
                )
                self.dirty = True
                return

            entries = data.get("entries")
            if not isinstance(entries, dict):
                raise ValueError("cache entries are not a dictionary")

            for path, entry in entries.items():
                if self._valid_entry(entry):
                    self.entries[path] = entry
                else:
                    LOG.debug("Discarding corrupted cache entry: %s", path)
                    self.dirty = True

        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:
            LOG.warning("Cache is corrupted and will be discarded: %s", e)
            self.entries = {}
            self.dirty = True

    @staticmethod
    def _valid_entry(entry):
        """Return True if an entry has the expected structure."""
        return (
            isinstance(entry, dict)
            and isinstance(entry.get("path"), str)
            and isinstance(entry.get("file_hash"), str)
            and isinstance(entry.get("issues"), list)
            and isinstance(entry.get("metrics"), dict)
            and isinstance(entry.get("cached_at"), (int, float))
            and isinstance(entry.get("cache_key"), str)
        )

    def get(self, path):
        """Return the cached entry for ``path`` or None."""
        return self.entries.get(path)

    def __contains__(self, path):
        return path in self.entries

    def store(self, path, entry):
        """Store (or replace) a cache entry for ``path``."""
        self.entries[path] = entry
        self.dirty = True

    def remove(self, path):
        """Remove the cache entry for ``path``."""
        if path in self.entries:
            del self.entries[path]
            self.dirty = True

    def clear(self):
        """Remove all cached entries."""
        self.entries = {}
        self.dirty = True

    def save(self):
        """Write the cache to disk, creating the directory if needed."""
        if not self.entries and not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
            return False

        os.makedirs(self.cache_dir, exist_ok=True)
        data = {
            "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
            "cache_key": self.cache_key,
            "updated_at": time.time(),
            "entries": self.entries,
        }
        tmp_path = self.cache_path + ".tmp"
        try:
            with open(tmp_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                json.dump(data, f, sort_keys=True, indent=2)
            os.replace(tmp_path, self.cache_path)
        finally:
            if os.path.exists(tmp_path):
                try:
                    os.remove(tmp_path)
                except OSError:
                    pass
        self.dirty = False
        self._enforce_size_limit()
        return True

    def _enforce_size_limit(self):
        """If the cache exceeds ``size_limit``, drop oldest entries."""
        if not self.size_limit:
            return
        if not os.path.exists(self.cache_path):
            return

        while self.entries and os.path.getsize(self.cache_path) > self.size_limit:
            oldest = min(
                self.entries,
                key=lambda p: self.entries[p].get("cached_at", 0),
            )
            del self.entries[oldest]
            self._write_cache_file()

    def _write_cache_file(self):
        """Write the current entries to disk."""
        os.makedirs(self.cache_dir, exist_ok=True)
        data = {
            "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
            "cache_key": self.cache_key,
            "updated_at": time.time(),
            "entries": self.entries,
        }
        tmp_path = self.cache_path + ".tmp"
        with open(tmp_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            json.dump(data, f, sort_keys=True, indent=2)
        os.replace(tmp_path, self.cache_path)
        self.dirty = False

    def list_files(self):
        """Return a sorted list of paths present in the cache."""
        return sorted(self.entries.keys())

    def prune(self, days):
        """Remove entries older than ``days`` days.

        :param days: Age threshold in days
        :return: Number of entries removed
        """
        if days is None or days < 0:
            return 0
        cutoff = time.time() - (days * 86400)
        removed = 0
        for path in list(self.entries.keys()):
            if self.entries[path].get("cached_at", 0) < cutoff:
                del self.entries[path]
                removed += 1
                self.dirty = True
        if removed:
            self.save()
        return removed

    def stats(self):
        """Return a dictionary of cache statistics."""
        size = (
            os.path.getsize(self.cache_path)
            if os.path.exists(self.cache_path)
            else 0
        )
        return {
            "cache_dir": self.cache_dir,
            "cache_file_size_bytes": size,
            "cached_files": len(self.entries),
            "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
        }

    def export(self, fileobj):
        """Export the cache to a JSON file object."""
        data = {
            "format_version": FORMAT_VERSION,
            "cache_key": self.cache_key,
            "exported_at": time.time(),
            "entries": self.entries,
        }
        json.dump(data, fileobj, sort_keys=True, indent=2)

    def import_cache(self, path):
        """Import and merge a previously exported cache file.

        Incompatible format versions and malformed input are discarded
        gracefully.
        """
        try:
            with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                data = json.load(f)
        except (OSError, ValueError) as e:
            LOG.warning("Ignoring malformed cache import %s: %s", path, e)
            return

        if not isinstance(data, dict):
            LOG.warning("Ignoring malformed cache import: %s", path)
            return
        if data.get("format_version") != FORMAT_VERSION:
            LOG.warning(
                "Ignoring cache import with incompatible format version: %s",
                path,
            )
            return

        entries = data.get("entries")
        if not isinstance(entries, dict):
            LOG.warning("Ignoring malformed cache import: %s", path)
            return

        merged = 0
        for entry_path, entry in entries.items():
            if self._valid_entry(entry):
                self.entries[entry_path] = entry
                merged += 1
            else:
                LOG.warning("Ignoring invalid cache entry from %s", path)
        if merged:
            self.dirty = True
            self.save()


def clear_cache_dir(cache_dir):
    """Remove the cache directory.  No-op if the directory is missing."""
    if os.path.isdir(cache_dir):
        shutil.rmtree(cache_dir)

                    new_files_list = [

new = '''    def run_tests(self, cache=None, force_rescan=False):
        """Runs through all files in the scope

        :param cache: Optional :class:`BanditCache` used for incremental
            analysis
        :param force_rescan: If True, bypass cache lookups but still store
            results
        :return: -
        """
        self.cache = cache
        self.cache_total_files = len(self.files_list)

        cached_scores = {}
        scan_scores = {}
        scan_cache_data = {}

        files_to_scan = self.files_list
        if cache is not None:
            files_to_scan, cached_scores = self._prepare_cache(
                cache, force_rescan
            )

        # if we have problems with a file, we'll remove it from the files_list
        # and add it to the skipped list instead
        new_files_list = list(self.files_list)
        if (
            len(files_to_scan) > PROGRESS_THRESHOLD
            and LOG.getEffectiveLevel() <= logging.INFO
        ):
            files = progress.track(files_to_scan)
        else:
            files = files_to_scan

        for count, fname in enumerate(files):
            LOG.debug("working on file : %s", fname)
            self.files_scanned.append(fname)

            try:
                if fname == "-":
                    open_fd = os.fdopen(sys.stdin.fileno(), "rb", 0)
                    fdata = io.BytesIO(open_fd.read())
                    new_files_list = [
                        "<stdin>" if x == "-" else x for x in new_files_list
                    ]
                    self._parse_file(
                        "<stdin>",
                        fdata,
                        new_files_list,
                        scan_scores,
                        scan_cache_data,
                    )
                else:
                    with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
                        self._parse_file(
                            fname,
                            fdata,
                            new_files_list,
                            scan_scores,
                            scan_cache_data,
                        )
            except OSError as e:
                self.skipped.append((fname, e.strerror))
                new_files_list.remove(fname)

        # reflect any files which may have been skipped
        self.files_list = new_files_list

        if cache is not None:
            self._store_cache_entries(cache, scan_cache_data)
            cache.save()

        # Build scores in files_list order so verbose output stays aligned.
        self.scores = []
        for fname in self.files_list:
            if fname in cached_scores:
                self.scores.append(cached_scores[fname])
            elif fname in scan_scores:
                self.scores.append(scan_scores[fname])
            else:
                self.scores.append(self._empty_score())

        self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_hits"] = self.cache_hits
        self.metrics.data["_totals"]["cache_misses"] = self.cache_misses

        # do final aggregation of metrics
        self.metrics.aggregate()

        try:
            # parse the current file
            data = fdata.read()
            lines = data.splitlines()
            self.metrics.begin(fname)
            self.metrics.count_locs(lines)
            # nosec_lines is a dict of line number -> set of tests to ignore
            #                                         for the line
            nosec_lines = dict()
            try:
                fdata.seek(0)
                tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)

                if not self.ignore_nosec:
                    for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
                        if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
                            nosec_lines[lineno] = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)

            except tokenize.TokenError:
                pass
            score = self._execute_ast_visitor(fname, fdata, data, nosec_lines)
            self.scores.append(score)
            self.metrics.count_issues([score])
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            sys.exit(2)
        except SyntaxError:
            self.skipped.append(

new2 = '''    def _parse_file(
        self,
        fname,
        fdata,
        new_files_list,
        scan_scores=None,
        scan_cache_data=None,
    ):
        def _record_scan(skipped=False, skip_reason=None, score=None):
            if scan_cache_data is not None and fname != "<stdin>":
                scan_cache_data[fname] = {
                    "score": score,
                    "skipped": skipped,
                    "skip_reason": skip_reason,
                }

        try:
            # parse the current file
            data = fdata.read()
            lines = data.splitlines()
            self.metrics.begin(fname)
            self.metrics.count_locs(lines)
            # nosec_lines is a dict of line number -> set of tests to ignore
            #                                         for the line
            nosec_lines = dict()
            try:
                fdata.seek(0)
                tokens = tokenize.tokenize(fdata.readline)

                if not self.ignore_nosec:
                    for toktype, tokval, (lineno, _), _, _ in tokens:
                        if toktype == tokenize.COMMENT:
                            nosec_lines[lineno] = _parse_nosec_comment(tokval)

            except tokenize.TokenError:
                pass
            score = self._execute_ast_visitor(fname, fdata, data, nosec_lines)
            if scan_scores is not None:
                scan_scores[fname] = score
            _record_scan(score=score)
            self.metrics.count_issues([score])
        except KeyboardInterrupt:
            sys.exit(2)
        except SyntaxError:
            self.skipped.append(
                (fname, "syntax error while parsing AST from file")
            )
            new_files_list.remove(fname)
            _record_scan(
                skipped=True,
                skip_reason="syntax error while parsing AST from file",
            )
        except Exception as e:
            LOG.error(
                "Exception occurred when executing tests against %s.", fname
            )
            if not LOG.isEnabledFor(logging.DEBUG):
                LOG.error(
                    \'Run "bandit --debug %s" to see the full traceback.\', fname
                )

            self.skipped.append((fname, "exception while scanning file"))
            new_files_list.remove(fname)
            _record_scan(
                skipped=True, skip_reason="exception while scanning file"
            )
            LOG.debug("  Exception string: %s", e)
            LOG.debug("  Exception traceback: %s", traceback.format_exc())
'''

MESSAGE
CALL s30_c0 TOOL_ID call_00_Gg0FPWRE7AaYkmiITB5O6064

cd /app && python - <<'PY'
path = 'bandit/core/manager.py'
src = open(path).read()

marker = "def _get_files_from_dir("
idx = src.index(marker)
insert = '''    def _prepare_cache(self, cache, force_rescan):
        """Decide which files to scan and load valid cached results.

        :param cache: The :class:`BanditCache` to consult
        :param force_rescan: If True, ignore cached results for every file
        :return: A tuple of ``(files_to_scan, cached_scores)``
        """
        files_to_scan = []
        cached_scores = {}
        path_to_fname = {}
        fname_to_path = {}

        for fname in self.files_list:
            path = os.path.abspath(fname)
            path_to_fname[path] = fname
            fname_to_path[fname] = path

        statuses = {}
        for fname in self.files_list:
            path = fname_to_path[fname]
            if fname in ("-", "<stdin>"):
                statuses[path] = "not_cached"
                continue
            if force_rescan:
                statuses[path] = "not_cached"
                continue
            entry = cache.get(path)
            if entry is None:
                statuses[path] = "not_cached"
                continue
            if entry.get("cache_key") != cache.cache_key:
                statuses[path] = "config_changed"
                continue
            if self._cache_entry_expired(entry, cache.expiry_days):
                statuses[path] = "expired"
                continue
            if not self._file_unchanged(path, entry):
                statuses[path] = "file_changed"
                continue
            statuses[path] = "valid"

        # Build a reverse dependency graph and propagate invalidation through
        # it.  A visited set makes circular imports safe.
        dependents = collections.defaultdict(list)
        for entry_path, entry in cache.entries.items():
            for dep in entry.get("dependencies") or []:
                dependents[dep].append(entry_path)

        invalid_paths = set()

        def _invalidate(path):
            if path in invalid_paths:
                return
            invalid_paths.add(path)
            for dependent in dependents.get(path, []):
                _invalidate(dependent)

        for path, status in statuses.items():
            if status != "valid":
                _invalidate(path)

        paths_to_remove = []
        for fname in self.files_list:
            path = fname_to_path[fname]
            if fname in ("-", "<stdin>"):
                files_to_scan.append(fname)
                self._record_cache_miss("not_cached")
                continue
            status = statuses[path]
            if status == "valid" and path not in invalid_paths:
                entry = cache.get(path)
                if entry.get("skipped"):
                    # Restore the skipped status but keep the file out of the
                    # active file list, matching non-incremental behavior.
                    self.skipped.append(
                        (fname, entry.get("skip_reason") or "cached skipped")
                    )
                    self.files_cached.append(fname)
                    self.cache_hits += 1
                    paths_to_remove.append(fname)
                else:
                    self._load_cached_file(fname, path, entry, cached_scores)
            else:
                reason = status if status != "valid" else "file_changed"
                files_to_scan.append(fname)
                self._record_cache_miss(reason)

        for fname in paths_to_remove:
            self.files_list.remove(fname)

        self.cache_total_files = len(self.files_list) + len(paths_to_remove)
        return files_to_scan, cached_scores

    @staticmethod
    def _cache_entry_expired(entry, expiry_days):
        """Return True if a cache entry is older than ``expiry_days``.

        A value of ``0`` expires every entry.
        """
        if expiry_days == 0:
            return True
        cached_at = entry.get("cached_at", 0)
        return time.time() - cached_at > expiry_days * 86400

    @staticmethod
    def _file_unchanged(path, entry):
        """Return True if the file's current hash matches the entry."""
        try:
            return b_cache.file_hash(path) == entry.get("file_hash")
        except OSError:
            return False

    def _record_cache_miss(self, reason):
        """Record a cache miss and its invalidation reason."""
        self.cache_misses += 1
        if reason in self.invalidation_counts:
            self.invalidation_counts[reason] += 1
        else:
            self.invalidation_counts["not_cached"] += 1
        self.invalidation_reasons[reason] = (
            self.invalidation_reasons.get(reason, 0) + 1
        )

    def _load_cached_file(self, fname, path, entry, cached_scores):
        """Load results for a file from the cache."""
        self.files_cached.append(fname)
        self.cache_hits += 1

        issue_dicts = entry.get("issues") or []
        for issue_dict in issue_dicts:
            try:
                self.results.append(issue.issue_from_dict(issue_dict))
            except Exception:
                LOG.debug(
                    "Discarding invalid cached issue for %s", fname
                )

        metrics_data = entry.get("metrics")
        if isinstance(metrics_data, dict):
            self.metrics.data[fname] = metrics_data

        cached_scores[fname] = self._score_from_issues(issue_dicts)

    @staticmethod
    def _empty_score():
        """Return an all-zero score dict."""
        return {
            "SEVERITY": [0] * len(b_constants.RANKING),
            "CONFIDENCE": [0] * len(b_constants.RANKING),
        }

    @staticmethod
    def _score_from_issues(issue_dicts):
        """Reconstruct a score dict from cached issue dictionaries."""
        score = {
            "SEVERITY": [0] * len(b_constants.RANKING),
            "CONFIDENCE": [0] * len(b_constants.RANKING),
        }
        for idict in issue_dicts:
            try:
                sev = idict["issue_severity"]
                conf = idict["issue_confidence"]
                sev_idx = b_constants.RANKING.index(sev)
                conf_idx = b_constants.RANKING.index(conf)
                score["SEVERITY"][sev_idx] += b_constants.RANKING_VALUES[sev]
                score["CONFIDENCE"][conf_idx] += b_constants.RANKING_VALUES[conf]
            except (KeyError, ValueError):
                continue
        return score

    def _store_cache_entries(self, cache, scan_cache_data):
        """Store results for files that were scanned during this run."""
        issues_by_file = collections.defaultdict(list)
        for res in self.results:
            issues_by_file[res.fname].append(res)

        known_paths = {os.path.abspath(f) for f in self.files_list}
        known_paths.update(cache.entries.keys())

        for fname, info in scan_cache_data.items():
            if fname == "<stdin>":
                continue
            path = os.path.abspath(fname)
            try:
                with open(fname, "rb") as fdata:
                    data = fdata.read()
            except OSError:
                continue

            deps = self._resolve_import_dependencies(fname, data, known_paths)
            try:
                mtime = os.path.getmtime(fname)
            except OSError:
                mtime = time.time()

            entry = {
                "path": path,
                "cache_key": cache.cache_key,
                "file_hash": b_cache.hash_bytes(data),
                "file_size": len(data),
                "mtime": mtime,
                "cached_at": time.time(),
                "dependencies": sorted(deps),
                "skipped": bool(info.get("skipped")),
                "skip_reason": info.get("skip_reason"),
                "issues": [
                    i.as_dict(with_code=True)
                    for i in issues_by_file.get(fname, [])
                ],
                "metrics": self.metrics.data.get(fname, {}),
            }
            cache.store(path, entry)

    def _resolve_import_dependencies(self, fname, data, known_paths):
        """Resolve a scanned file's imports to cached file paths."""
        imports = _get_imports_from_source(data)
        deps = set()
        for module_name in imports:
            rel = module_name.replace(".", "/")
            for known in known_paths:
                if known.endswith("/" + rel + ".py") or known.endswith(
                    "/" + rel + "/__init__.py"
                ):
                    deps.add(known)
                    break
        return deps


def _get_imports_from_source(data):
    """Return the set of module names imported by the given source bytes."""
    imports = set()
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(data)
    except (SyntaxError, ValueError):
        return imports

    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            for alias in node.names:
                imports.add(alias.name)
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
            if node.module:
                imports.add(node.module)
    return imports


'''
